AUTOMATION Center

-- 14 of 416 --

1. Introduction
The AUTOMATION Center is a software application that makes repetitive, manual steps
easy to automate.
OPEN MIND Technologies AG has developed a seamlessly integrated, high-performance
automation tool that can be used to automate or standardize almost all functions in
hy-
perMILL®. Workflows can be combined while also applying script control.
The following features distinguish the software:
• Full access to almost all CAD and CAM functions
• Additional Functions
• Customizable scripts and input instructions
• Parameterizable scripts
• Make scripts easy to understand with custom images
• Downstream processes remain unaffected by the automation and can be performed
without restrictions
• No programming language necessary
• High degree of flexibility and universal usability
• Short training period
Licensing model
Version Acquisition Target group
AUTOMATION Center BASIC All customers
Generally integrated, for prepara-
tory routine processes
AUTOMATION Center ADVANCED Purchasea. Script develop-
er
AUTOMATION Center RUNTIME Script user
AUTOMATION Center Customer Solution Cannot be purchased. For the
purpose of developing scripts as
a service of OPEN MIND
a.The software is essentially offered in two versions, whereby the Runtime license can be used together with the
Advanced license.
AUTOMATION Center
1

-- 15 of 416 --

2. User interface
AUTOMATION Center allows commands to be called from
hyperMILL® CAD and CAM. In
addition, there are further additional commands that are only available in AUTOMATION
Center.
Procedure
A script combines functions into a sequence, to be run from top to bottom. The script
contains components, which in turn contain functions. Functions are available as template
commands to be inserted into a script. Components can contain subcomponents.
1. As a prerequisite, check under hyperMILL → Setup → Settings in Database whether the
databases, in particular the tool database, are selected correctly.
NOTE
Project-specific organization of data is necessary for working with
the AUTOMATION Center. For this reason, the Projekt option is
enabled under Document → Directories in the hyperMILL → Setup →
Settings function.
2. Open script management . Create a new script . Enter a name. The script is
still empty. Close the dialog.
3. Prepare a script in definition mode . To do so, create a component or a
subcomponent in a component in the left column. Arrange the functions in the
desired sequence.
To do so, select the function or pre-configured component in the right column and
insert it in the left column using the button. This is also possible by double-clicking
with the left mouse button or dragging and dropping.
Close the dialog when the script is complete.
4. Specify the parameters for each function in the parameters table in the main dialog. To
do so, switch to parameter definition mode . Disable this mode once all parameters
have been defined.
5. Optionally, in the node connection editor, create the parameter constraints from one
function to another function using leader lines.
6. Test the script and optimize if necessary. To do so, run the script in steps or in its
entirety . A single step is also possible by double-clicking the function with the left
mouse button.
7. Once the script is ready, it can optionally be inserted in the menu and/or toolbar. Go to
script management and open the toolbar definition dialog .
8. Scripts can be run from hyperMILL → Automation → ....

Control processing of the active script:
Continuous mode
Continuous mode runs through the entire script until a stop command is reached
or input is necessary.
Step mode
Step mode runs only the selected steps.
Pause processing.
Processing is interrupted at the current position. Processing can usually be
restarted by clicking the button again.
Stop script execution
Stopping can be switched on and off for each step in the script. Continuous
mode stops at the step marked with a stop.
Shows the image from the image area at a larger size.

scripts is displayed 1 	. Select and apply a script 2 	.

Switch to definition mode in the main dialog using the button. Prepare a script. All
functions which can be inserted into a script are listed in a tree structure on the right side of
the dialog, and are known as template functions. Already pre-configured scripts also appear
there.
The scope of available template commands and pre-configured scripts depends on the
purchased license.

Parameter definition
Fill out all parameters for the function assigned to a script step. Select the script step within
the script tree structure to be processed. Enable editing of the parameter definition using
the button in the main dialog.
1
2
3
4
5
The options in the input field are also processed from top to bottom. This is used for some
functions, for example, in the Set layer visible/invisible function.
1 	Enter a short description of the purpose of the function, to be displayed in the
script overview.
2 	Input field
Enter values. Depending on the function, a string or a number may be permissi-
ble.
The colored background of a cell provides information about its function:
• Cream yellow: A value must be entered in any case.
• White: Input can be made by the user during the script run if the script is
designed to stop for the input of a value.
• Grey: Entering a value is optional.
To enter a vector, use a semicolon as the separator, for example, 0;0;1.
A parameter of the main script can be referenced in a subscript. Even if the main
script has not been executed, it is possible to view the referenced parameter by
resting the mouse pointer in the subscript.
3 	Drop-down list
In certain functions, a drop-down list allows the user to select from a list of
already existing entries, such as a machine or a material, for example.
AUTOMATION Center
8

-- 22 of 416 --

4 	Input button
All table cells with a double border are input buttons. Click the button and then
select an entity to automatically transfer color, layer or vector. This can be used
to avoid manual entry of an RGB value, layer or vector.
5 	Link string
Clicking a table cell in the first table column copies a link to the information
in the table cell to the clipboard. This link string makes it possible to use this
information in other locations. It is only possible to modify the information at the
original location.
Predefined parameter table
Processing of entries for options in functions. Insert using arrow icon on the right in table
row. Can be used for each option to carry out ‘local’ calculations, for example, to round
values, read in translations, and so on.
Text editor
A text editor is located below the parameter table. Double-clicking a value inserts it into the
text editor. Each inline parameter encloses the content in its brackets.
Inline functions
An example selection:
biggest Use biggest value
biggest(10:20:30) results in 30
calc The result of the calculation is always a whole number.
calc(1+2)
decimal Number of decimal places
decimal()
file Separate the pure file from a complete file name.
file()
ge Value greater or equal to
ge(10:10 ; true:false) results in true
gt Value greater than
gt(10:20 ; true:false) results in false

Layer loop
Contained functions are executed for the filtered layer, for example, for the respec-
tive current layer $CURRENT_LAYER#.
Filter layer: Filter instruction, for example, enter a part of layer names.
Counted loops
Contained functions are executed x times. The current number of passes is in
$CURRENT_LOOP_INDEX#.
Number of repeats: Enter a number.
Counted loops 2
Contained functions are executed Y times, for example, for setting up a double
loop, where a loop is passed through X times. And inside, a second loop is
passed through Y times for each pass. The current number of passes is in $CUR-
RENT_LOOP_INDEX2#.
Number of repeats 2: Enter a number.
Joblist loop
Contained functions are executed for joblists that meet the condition.
Include spindles: Include main spindle and counter spindle jobs for turning.
Job loop and Job Loop 2
Contained functions are executed for the filtered job, for example, for the respec-
tive current job $CURRENT_JOB#.
Joblist filter: Filter instruction, for example, enter a part of a joblist name.
Sub job list filter: Filter instruction, for example, enter a part of a sub-joblist name.
Filter jobs: Filter instruction, for example, enter a part of a job name.
Only compound jobs: Optionally execute only for compound jobs.
Only active jobs: Optionally execute only for active jobs. A job is active when the
Create NC file option of the General dialog page is enabled in a job.
Tool filter: Run through for milling tools, turning tools or all tools.
Compound job: Enter compound job.
Include spindles: Include main spindle and counter spindle jobs for turning.

Job tool loop
Runs through all jobs and makes one pass for each existing tool.
Joblist filter: Filter instruction, for example, enter a part of a joblist name.
Sub job list filter: Filter instruction, for example, enter a part of a sub-joblist name.
Only active jobs: Optionally execute only for active jobs. A job is active when the
Create NC file option of the General dialog page is enabled in a job.
Order: Run through the order of the NC number from the tool definition in ascend-
ing or descending order of appearance in the job list.
Include spindles: Yes / No
Tool loop
Contained functions are executed for the filtered tool, for example, for the respec-
tive current tool $CURRENT_TOOL#.
Order: Run through the order of the NC number from the tool definition in ascend-
ing or descending order of appearance in the job list.
Joblist filter: Filter instruction, for example, enter a part of a joblist name.
Tool filter: Run through for milling tools, turning tools or all tools.
Used tools only: Only run through for used tools.
Order: Processing takes place in the selected order of appearance or in the
selected NC number order.
Feature loop
Contained functions are executed for the filtered feature, for example, for the
respective current feature $CURRENT_FEATURE_LOOP_INDEX#.
Feature filter: Only run through for drill features or pocket features.
Frame loop
Contained functions are executed for the filtered
hyperMILL® frames, origins, or
both sub-types, such as for the current frame $CURRENT_FRAME#.
Sub type: Execute for frames, origins, or both sub-types.

Entity loop
Go through all entities that are filtered out using Entities color and Entities layer.
Filter type: Process a selected entity type (for example, faces, solids, curves,

Bookmark loop

Filter workplane: Filter instruction, for example, enter a part of the name of a
workplane.
Text loop
Write all Excel worksheets to a text file. This text file can be used to create a
combo box or a loop. Set up a loop with theRead Excel sheets into file (page 372)
command.
Use Folder and Filename to specify the text file.
2024
Conditional container
A stop occurs before the component container is processed. The user must decide
whether a condition applies. If the user chooses ‘Yes’, the functions within the con-
tainer are executed. If the user selects ‘No’, the container is skipped (in contrast to
conditions in other types of components, where there is no compulsory stop).
Question: Enter a question.
Condition: Check whether a script variable is <, >, = or ≠ to a value.
Error level: Specify which information should be displayed as a message and at what time.
• Off: No messages are output.
• Basic: Messages are only output for serious errors.
• Input: Messages are only output if the starting conditions necessary for the script step do
not exist or are not correct.
• Input / output: Messages are only output if the starting conditions necessary for the script
step do not exist or are not correct or no result or no correct result can be generated.
• Detailed: Debugging information is also output for testing purposes during software devel-
opment.
Always visible: Specify whether an inactive component should be visible as a result of a set
condition. Inactive components are normally hidden.

Node connection editor
In the node connection editor 8 	, visualize and create the parameter constraints from one
function to another function using leader lines. The procedure is inspired by the ‘node pro-
gramming’ principle (Node based programming). The functions are considered as nodes.
To create a connection, hold down the left mouse button and drag the mouse from one
connection point to another. To remove a connection, highlight it at the target point and
press the DELETE key.

representation)

When the mouse touches the start point, an existing connection is highlighted in color.
When the mouse touches a target point, the connection is highlighted in a different color.

Disable second script mode.
The nodes of a script or subscript are displayed one below the other. With-
out second script mode, the nodes in longer scripts are arranged in a zigzag
in order to save space.
In second script mode, a subscript can be displayed next to the main script.
This allows connections to be easily dragged from or to a subscript.
The Subscript option is also displayed. Select a subscript – provided that
subscripts are used.

Configuration
Process application
Runtime app folder: Select the folder in which scripts provided by OPEN MIND (Runtime or
Customer Solution Reference apps) are stored to display them in the Customer applications
branch. This selection overwrites the folder that has been selected in the default path.
Editor: Choose a text editor to edit text, for example, in a VBScript. If no selection is made,
the Notepad++notepad++.exe software is searched for first. Otherwise, the Windows Editor
is used.
Script base folder: Folder in which to store the scripts (in subfolders if necessary). In , you
can select from the history.
AUTOMATION Center
17

-- 31 of 416 --

Graphics editor: Select an image editing program.
Format: Control whether completed, licensed customer solutions should be displayed in
the format *.xml or *.hms. This setting can only be specified with a Developer license. No
change may be made with a BASIC license.
Functionality: Filter the scope of the functionality that is displayed in accordance with the
selected license.
Display command names in Execute mode: Optionally display the function names in Exe-
cute mode – useful for test scenarios.
Display hidden parameters: Also display the parameters from the parameter definition for
which the lock (in the first column of the table) is closed.
Ask reset runtime parameters: While a script is executed, settings are made that the script
remembers, such as the selection of a particular machine. To work with the original settings
of the script again, select this option. When the script is called again, a prompt appears
asking whether the settings should be reset.
Ask closing AUTOMATION Center: Optionally confirm the closure of the AUTOMATION
Center.
Enable subscripts: Enable the creation of subscripts.
Run inside AUTOMATION Center (no execute mode): For analysis purposes, the AUTO-
MATION Center is run with its dialogs to process a script.
Display text panel in definition mode: While defining a script, add a text field to the main dia-
log for documenting the respective script step or formulating an instruction when executing
the script.
Display text panel in execute mode: When executing a script, add the text field with the
optionally stored instruction to the dialog of the script step.
Update runtime scripts: A decision can be made whether to update existing local runtime
scripts from the server.
Ignore stop points: Ignore the stop points set for developing.
Script bookmarks: Bookmark script steps in an additionally displayed dialog area in order to
jump to a particular script step within the script by double-clicking the bookmark. All existing
bookmarks are displayed in the dialog – both from the master script and from its subscripts.
Bookmarks make it easy to jump back and forth between the different scripts.
Create a script bookmark for a script step. Select the script step beforehand.
Delete a script bookmark. Select the bookmark beforehand.
Use short script variables: For a better overview, the display of the script variable used
is temporarily and automatically replaced by %P1%, %P2%, %P3%, ... during parameter
definition. These short script variables are only valid for a short time at the time of input.
The normal script variables are always saved.
2024
Note
Always load the CAD model into the document before executing a script.
The Undo and Redo functions are not supported. Useful background information: CAD
entities such as the geometry that specifies the fixture are converted into CAM entities.

Default folder for the AUTOMATION Center
Company-wide default setting
Select a folder under hyperMILL → Setup → Settings in Application under Default paths
in the AUTOMATION Center branch. It is advisable to set up the default path on the
AUTOMATION Center server. A search is made there for the following data:
• ...\Apps: Scripts provided by OPEN MIND or custom scripts to be displayed in the
Customer applications branch. Preset folder. Another folder can be prioritized in the
configuration.
NOTE
These template commands and pre-specified script components may
only be referenced or inserted once per script.
• ...\CLAMPS: Fixtures that are stored in subfolders for each fixture system. The fixture
systems can be optionally arranged according to machine.
• ...\COMPONENTS: Component file folder for exported components or subcomponents.
2024
• ...\DATABASE: When working with several, different versions of AUTOMATION Center
databases, for example Color_table.xml, MacroDB.db, ToolDB.db and Virtual_tool.vtx, it
is advisable to store them here.
• ...\EXPORTED: Custom, compressed scripts for data exchange or data backup
• ...\FEATURE: Feature file folder for exported features
• ...\LIBRARY: CAD data categories with CAD model data
• ...\MULTIOFFSET: .cfg definition files for milling area multiple allowances
• ...\REPORTS: *.xsl report layouts
• ...\STOCKS: *.cfg stock definition files that are stored in subfolders for each stock catego-
ry.
• ...\variants: Global script data that is stored in subfolders for each script. Each time
the AUTOMATION Center is started, the local scripts are updated if necessary. Switch
off these updates using the Update runtime scripts option in Configuration → Process
application.
• ...\VB SCRIPTS: Store VBScript files categorizedin subfolders.
• ...\PYTHON SCRIPTS: Store Python script files categorizedin subfolders.
Local default settings
In the folder C:\Users\Public\Documents\OPEN MIND\USERS\[Username]\AutomationCen-
ter\...
• ...\variants: Local storage location for scripts. Each time the AUTOMATION Center is
started, the local scripts are updated, if necessary, from the ...\variants folder of the folder
selected under hyperMILL → Setup → Settings in Application under Default paths in the
AUTOMATION Center branch.
Under Configuration → Process application → Script base folder, select the folder in which
scripts provided by OPEN MIND are stored to display them in theCustomer applications
branch. This selection overwrites the folder that has been selected in the default path.

Start composite UI
Combine multiple script steps in one dialog.
Template commands → User interface → Start composite UI
Advanced
Combine multiple script steps in one dialog. However, only if the script is executed in
Execute mode. To do this, insert this function into a script at the beginning of the required
area.
There are two variants:
• Combine all inputs and outputs in one dialog up to End composite UI. All inserted script
steps are processed. To do this, insert End composite UI at the end of the area.
• Combine all inputs up to the next stop for an input. Processing does not take place.
Start composite UI determines the preset, first image. Each script step can have its own
image. An image comes from End composite UI if no own images have been inserted.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
The character | is not allowed to be used. A * is replaced by the original command name.

Curve layer: Select the curve layer. Only uses curves that are assigned to this layer.

tance. Enter a vector for the direction.
Vector angle tolerance: Set the focus for the search in the direction specified by the refer-
ence vector as the angle value.
X->, Y->, and Z->: The X, Y, and Z coordinates of the initial point are output.
X MIN->, Y MIN->, and Z MIN->: The X, Y, and Z coordinates of the point with the minimal
distance are output.
Found vector: The determined vector between the two points is output.

Conditional container
A stop occurs before the component container is processed. The user must decide
whether a condition applies. If the user chooses ‘Yes’, the functions within the con-
tainer are executed. If the user selects ‘No’, the container is skipped (in contrast to
conditions in other types of components, where there is no compulsory stop).

• Off: No messages are output.
• Basic: Messages are only output for serious errors.
• Input: Messages are only output if the starting conditions necessary for the script step do
not exist or are not correct.
• Input / output: Messages are only output if the starting conditions necessary for the script
step do not exist or are not correct or no result or no correct result can be generated.

Read translation file
Read in translations for script steps.
Template commands → Helper functions → Read translation file
Advanced
Read in translations for script steps.
Only the languages offered for
hyperMILL®and their language codes are supported:
de: German, fr: French, it: Italian, nl: Dutch, es: Spanish, cs: Czech, pl: Polish, ru: Russian,
sl: Slovenian, ja: Japanese, ko: Korean. zh_CN: Simplified Chinese, zh_TW: Traditional
Chinese, pt_BR: Brazilian Portuguese, tr: Turkish.
Prepare the translations in a text file in *.loc format. The text file must contain the transla-
tions in the structure ID[TAB]TRANSLATION. Use a new line for each ID. There must be a
tab between the ID and translation!
Example 18. Localization files for German, English and Spanish
project.de.loc
id01 Meine Elemente rot einfärben
id02 Meine Elemente blau einfärben
project.en.loc
id01 Colouring my entities in red
id02 Colouring my entities in blue
project.es.loc
id01 Colorear mis entidades en rojo
id02 Colorear mis entidades en rojo
Use the translation, for example, in Command name, for descriptions, comments, and so
on by entering the call local([Developer_name]|#[LOC_ID]). A name must be entered as the
work title for [Developer_name] in case there is no localization file. For [LOC_ID], enter the
relevant ID of the *.loc file with the translation.
Example 19. Adopt localization
Table 1. Call
Command name: local(Colouring entity|#id01)
Table 2. Result for Spanish
Nombre de función: Colorear mis entidades en rojo

-- 90 of 416 --

Example 21. Only make a few layers visible
1. Use the first Layer invisible option to generally disable all layers, that is, make all
entities invisible. To do this, use the asterisk (*) symbol as a wildcard for all layer
names.
2. In the case of the Layer visible option, it is now simply necessary to enter all the layers
that should be enabled again. Separate multiple layer names with a semicolon ;.
Example 22. Only make a few layers invisible
1. Use the Layer visible option to generally enable all layers, that is, make all entities
visible. To do this, use the asterisk (*) symbol as a wildcard for all layer names.
2. In the case of the second Layer invisible option, it is now simply necessary to enter
all the layers that should be disabled again. Separate multiple layer names with a
semicolon ;.

click the button in the table cell and then select a layer entity in the graphic area.

function.
Layer visible: Enter or select which layer should be visible and should therefore be evalu-
ated. The asterisk (*) symbol can be used as a wildcard. To make a selection, first click the
button in the table cell and then select a layer entity in the graphic area.
The layer visibility control is performed on reaching the function and not on executing the
function.
Layer invisible: Enter or select which layer should not be visible and should therefore not
be evaluated. The asterisk (*) symbol can be used as a wildcard. To make a selection, first
click the button in the table cell and then select a layer entity in the graphic area.
The layer visibility control is performed on reaching the function and not on executing the
function.
Stop for input: Execution of the script is halted so that information can be entered.

to disable a specific selection of entities. Use the asterisk (*) symbol as a wildcard for all

Example 33. Only make one color visible
1. Use the first Color invisible option to generally disable all entities, that is, make all
entities invisible. To do this, use the asterisk (*) symbol as a wildcard for all colors.
2. In the case of the Color visible option, it is now simply necessary to enter the color that
should be enabled again.
Example 34. Only make one color invisible
1. Use the Color visible option to generally enable all colors, that is, make all entities
visible. To do this, use the asterisk (*) symbol as a wildcard for all colors.
2. For the second option Color invisible, only one color needs to be entered now that is to
be switched off again.

tion.

Dialog during the script run
During the script run, a dialog opens in which faces are selected interactively based on the
defined criteria. The dialog opens with the selection option preset in Selection constrain.
Select color. To do this, click one of the color faces offered. The number of color
faces offered depends on the setting in Count colors. The appearance of the
icon varies accordingly.
Single selection. Select a single face or color all preselected faces. The appear-
ance of the icon varies according to the current color.
Color faces in the course of a constant fillet within a solid or a face model. Select
a face within the fillet.
Example 35.
Min. radius / Max. radius: Limit the course of the required fillet faces further by
specifying a minimum and a maximum radius.
Color faces in the course of a constant chamfer within a solid or a face model.
Select a face within the chamfer.

faces by specifying a maximum and a minimum diagonal length 1 	or using the

Save color set
Save the current color state of all faces under one name.
Template commands → Coloring → Save color set
Advanced
For each face entity, the current color is saved based on its ID. This snapshot of the model
color state can be saved as a color set under a name.
Example 41.
As a service provider, use Save color set to save a color state supplied by the customer
in the model in order to be able to make color changes that are necessary for automation.

gramming process, restore the original state with the Activate color set function.

Coloring planes
Search for and color plane level.
Template commands → Coloring → Coloring planes
Advanced
Search for and color one or several coplanar plane levels based on the Z direction of the
reference coordinate system.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
The character | is not allowed to be used. A * is replaced by the original command name.
Reference coordinate system: Select the NCS or the workplane as the reference.
Reference axis: Select a reference axis as the normal for the planes that are being
searched for.
Optionally enter either a tolerance angle or a min./max. tolerance angle:
Tolerance angle: Enter an angle for the permissible deviation of planes from the desired
plane normal direction.
Min. tolerance angle: Enter a minimum angle for the permissible deviation of planes from
the desired plane normal direction.
Max. tolerance angle: Enter a maximum angle for the permissible deviation of planes from
the desired plane normal direction.
Filter face normal in reference direction: Use the normals of the faces or, better still, the
solids to count the plane levels.
Face layer: Enter one or several layers that exist in the model whose faces should be
included. Separate multiple layer names with a semicolon ;, for example, Layer1; Layer2.
Optionally click the button in the table cell and select a face of the respective layer. The
layer name is transferred.
Face color: Enter an RGB color value for the faces that exist in the model that are to be
included. Optionally click the button in the table cell and select a face of the respective
color. The RGB color value is transferred.
Target layer: Enter or select a layer for the entities that are generated or modified with this
function.
Target color Enter or select a color with RGB values.
Min. value in reference axis direction: Entities below the minimum value in relation to the
reference axis are not considered. Enter a value.
Max. value in reference axis direction: Entities above the maximum value in relation to the
reference axis are not considered. Enter a value.
Min. face area: Only color faces that are larger than the minimum face area.
Max. face area: Only color faces that are smaller than the maximum face area.
Disable plane filter: Apply to all entities (for example, ‘slightly curved’ NURBS) or only to
plane faces.
Level: Select a plane level number to find a specific plane level. Here, 1 = first plane level; 2
= second plane level; ...

Example 43. Color electrode block

are colored.

Search equal rotational faces
Find and color cone, torus, or cylinder entities with the same geometric
properties.
Template commands → Coloring → Search equal rotational faces
Advanced
Search for all cone, torus or cylinder entities that have the same geometric properties as the
entity serving as the pattern.
The direction of the entities must also match.
The pattern entity is evaluated on the first call and the values for searching for further
entities are filled in. Based on this information, all identical segments are searched for on
the next call.
The pattern entity must be assigned a unique color. All the entities found are assigned the
same color as the pattern entity. Closed shapes are not allowed. Only specify the actual
faces as patterns.
AUTOMATION Center
126

-- 140 of 416 --

Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
The character | is not allowed to be used. A * is replaced by the original command name.
Color: Enter or select a color with RGB values.
Min. segment angle: Enter a minimum segment angle.
Reference diameter: Outputs the reference diameter of the pattern entity.
Cone angle: Outputs the cone angle of the pattern entity. The cone angle is 0° for cylinders.
Second radius: Outputs the second radius of the pattern entity.
Z value for reference diameter: Outputs the Z value for the position of the reference radius.
Type: Outputs the entity type that is determined. TORUS stands for torus-shaped segments
and CONE for conical segments. CONE is also output for cylinders – with a 0° cone angle.

Start Contains functions for generating joblists and for preparing the
hyperMILL® environment.
Set user database project
Select a database project.
Template commands → Start → Set user database project
Basic
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
The character | is not allowed to be used. A * is replaced by the original command name.
Database mode: Specify whether the application database project Application, user data-
base project User or the global database project Global should be used as the database, as
specified on the Database tab in hyperMILL → Setup → Settings.
Database project name: Enter the name of a user database project.
Start
Create a CAM project with a new joblist.
Template commands → Start → Start
Basic
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
The character | is not allowed to be used. A * is replaced by the original command name.
Joblist name: Enter a name for the joblist.
Milling area name: Enter or select a name for the milling area.
Machine: Select a machine. A defined machine is required.
Material: Select a material from existing material definitions in the active tool database.
NC filename: Enter an NC file name.
Compensated center path: Enable the option for the compensated center path.
Multiple origins: Enable the joblist option for multiple origins.
Allow counter spindle: Activate to be able to generate a job list structure for turning with
main and counter spindle. This consists of the two sub-job lists Main spindle (also seeNew
main spindle job (page 310)) and Counter spindle (also seeNew counter spindle job (page
311)) as well as the Transfer job for transferring the turning model between the main and
counter spindle.
Layer invisible: Enter or select which layer should not be visible and should therefore not
be evaluated. The asterisk (*) symbol can be used as a wildcard. To make a selection, first
click the button in the table cell and then select a layer entity in the graphic area.
The layer visibility control is performed on reaching the function and not on executing the
function.
Layer visible: Enter or select which layer should be visible and should therefore be evalu-
ated. The asterisk (*) symbol can be used as a wildcard. To make a selection, first click the
button in the table cell and then select a layer entity in the graphic area.
AUTOMATION Center
145

-- 159 of 416 --

The layer visibility control is performed on reaching the function and not on executing the
function.
Stop for input: Execution of the script is halted so that information can be entered.
Restore layer setting: Restore all layer settings to the state they were in before this function
was executed.

Milling area
Create a new milling area and add this area to the last joblist.
Template commands → Start → Milling area
Basic
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
The character | is not allowed to be used. A * is replaced by the original command name.
Milling area name: Enter or select a name for the milling area.
Tolerance: Enter the value for the resolution in the joblist.
Face layer: Enter one or several layers that exist in the model whose faces should be
included. Separate multiple layer names with a semicolon ;, for example, Layer1; Layer2.
Optionally click the button in the table cell and select a face of the respective layer. The
layer name is transferred.
Face color: Enter an RGB color value for the faces that exist in the model that are to be
included. Optionally click the button in the table cell and select a face of the respective
color. The RGB color value is transferred.
Layer invisible: Enter or select which layer should not be visible and should therefore not
be evaluated. The asterisk (*) symbol can be used as a wildcard. To make a selection, first
click the button in the table cell and then select a layer entity in the graphic area.
The layer visibility control is performed on reaching the function and not on executing the
function.
Layer visible: Enter or select which layer should be visible and should therefore be evalu-
ated. The asterisk (*) symbol can be used as a wildcard. To make a selection, first click the
button in the table cell and then select a layer entity in the graphic area.
The layer visibility control is performed on reaching the function and not on executing the
function.
Restore layer setting: Restore all layer settings to the state they were in before this function
was executed.
Prefer existing milling area: If already present, the milling area will be re-entered in the
joblist instead of being created again.
Type: Select from Face or Solid. For example, six faces or one solid are entered in the
milling area for a cube. It is useful to choose solids when associativity is required.
2024
Milling area multi offset
Create a milling area with multiple offsets on different face groups.
Template commands → Start → Milling area multi offset
Basic
The colors of the CAD model are used to specify different tolerances. A definition file can be
edited in a text editor and different offsets can be assigned.
AUTOMATION Center
149

-- 163 of 416 --

Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
The character | is not allowed to be used. A * is replaced by the original command name.
Milling area name: Enter or select a name for the milling area.
Definition files: In the drop-down menu, select a file from the list of files in the ...\MULTI-
OFFSET\ folder.
NOTE
An example is available in the ...\addins\hmAutoColor\Wizards\Automa-
tionCenter\OMGEAPP\includeStart\... installation folder. The name of the
example is ‘Multioffset.cfg’.
Save a definition file in the ...\MULTIOFFSET\ folder in order to make it
available to AUTOMATION Center. The folder is located in the defined
default path that is specified under hyperMILL → Setup → Settings in
Application under Default paths in the AUTOMATION Center branch.
Example 61. Example of the syntax of a text file for defining multiple offsets
*START
*TARGET:="GroupSurfs0"
*ALLOWANCE:="0.0"
*ORIG_COLOR1:="95;0;0"
*ORIG_COLOR2:="255;175;175"
*ORIG_COLOR3:="0;175;175"
*ORIG_COLOR4:="255;255;0"
*ORIG_COLOR5:="255;0;255"
*ORIG_COLOR6:="239;239;239"
*ORIG_COLOR7:="255;175;0"
*END
*START
*TARGET:="GroupSurfs1"
*ALLOWANCE:="0.5"
*ORIG_COLOR1:="225;180;180"
*ORIG_COLOR2:="255;128;0"
*ORIG_COLOR3:="80;143;12"
*ORIG_COLOR4:="0;207;111"
*ORIG_COLOR5:="210;210;255"
*ORIG_COLOR5:="168;122;168"
*END
Type: Select from Face or Solid. For example, six faces or one solid are entered in the
milling area for a cube. It is useful to choose solids when associativity is required.
2024
Add allowance group in millingarea
Add group of faces with separate allowance in the milling area.
Template commands → Start → Add allowance group in millingarea
Basic
AUTOMATION Center
150

-- 164 of 416 --

Create a normal milling area and link it to a joblist or job as usual. Call the Add allowance
group in millingarea function for each group to be defined. The faces can be filtered by
color, layer (also possible with *), tags and layer container. To define a group with a
different color or layer, add the selection to the existing group.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
The character | is not allowed to be used. A * is replaced by the original command name.
Milling area name: Enter or select a name for the milling area.
Group number: Enter the group number for the combination of ‘group name_group number’.
Group name: Enter group name.
Face layer: Enter one or several layers that exist in the model whose faces should be
included. Separate multiple layer names with a semicolon ;, for example, Layer1; Layer2.
Optionally click the button in the table cell and select a face of the respective layer. The
layer name is transferred.
Face color: Enter an RGB color value for the faces that exist in the model that are to be
included. Optionally click the button in the table cell and select a face of the respective
color. The RGB color value is transferred.
Tag name: Enter or select a tag name.
Tag value: Enter a value or text based on the underlying tag type.
Allowance: Enter the height of the allowance.
Add to selection: To define a group with a different color or layer, add the selection to the
existing group.

NCS Contains functions for generating and modifying the
hyperMILL® NCS.
NCS from workplane
Change an NCS position to the current position and orientation of the workplane.
Template commands → NCS → NCS from workplane
Basic
To achieve a correct result, a milling area is required on the current CAD model position.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
The character | is not allowed to be used. A * is replaced by the original command name.
Name of NCS: Enter a name.
Type: Select NCS or origin.
NCS position: Select a position from various positions of the milling area bounding box.
Possible positions:
• Bottom-Center
• Bottom-Center-Back
• Bottom-Center-Front
• Bottom-Center-Left
• Bottom-Center-Right
• Bottom-Left-Back
• Bottom-Left-Front
• Bottom-Right-Back
• Bottom-Right-Front
• Top-Center
• Top-Center-Back
• Top-Center-Front
• Top-Center-Left
• Top-Center-Right
• Top-Left-Back
• Top-Left-Front
• Top-Right-Back
• Top-Right-Front
• Workplane
Direction: Select a direction for the Z axis. The direction is used for the NCS in the joblist.
Keep axis: Select an axis for which a current position is to be maintained.
Fixed X Position: The X value of the position is not changed. Therefore, if the X and Y
values are fixed, only the Z value will be modified.
Fixed Y position: The Y value of the position is not changed.
Fixed Z Position: The Z value of the position is not changed.
Stop for input: Execution of the script is halted so that information can be entered.

NCS best fit
Calculate an NCS based on the smallest dimensions of the CAD model.
Template commands → NCS → NCS best fit
Basic
AUTOMATION Center
157

-- 171 of 416 --

Calculate an NCS based on the smallest dimensions of the CAD model. The position of the
CAD model to the world coordinate system does not matter.
A joblist with a milling area is required. The Z direction is based on the current view in the
graphic area.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
The character | is not allowed to be used. A * is replaced by the original command name.
Name of NCS: Enter a name.
With a pure 3D free-form model, no precise orientation using special planes is needed, that
is, no fine adjustment to planes is required. A fine adjustment is required for a prismatic
CAD model. A planes search takes place in the tolerance range. The planes search can be
optimized if a color and a layer are already established for a plane to be used:
Fine adjust to planes: Specify whether the NCS should be finely adjusted using a plane with
a specific color. To use the fine adjustment, a suitable, colored, plane face must exist.
Fine adjust color: Specify a color for the fine adjustment using a plane. The color of the
plane should only be provided for this purpose.
Layer: Enter a layer on which the entities to be used are located. Enter a layer name or
select an entity on this layer. The ID of an entity can also be entered instead. The input
must start with ID:, for example, ID:1234.
NCS position: Select a position from various positions of the milling area bounding box.
Possible positions:
• Bottom-Center
• Bottom-Center-Back
• Bottom-Center-Front
• Bottom-Center-Left
• Bottom-Center-Right
• Bottom-Left-Back
• Bottom-Left-Front
• Bottom-Right-Back
• Bottom-Right-Front
• Top-Center
• Top-Center-Back
• Top-Center-Front
• Top-Center-Left
• Top-Center-Right
• Top-Left-Back
• Top-Left-Front
• Top-Right-Back
• Top-Right-Front
• Workplane
Stop for input: Execution of the script is halted so that information can be entered.
NCS 2 faces
Generate an NCS by selecting the position and by clicking two faces.
Template commands → NCS → NCS 2 faces
Basic
A joblist with a milling area is required.
To execute the function properly, it is necessary to display the image field. There, follow the
instructions for clicking the faces.
This command is only suitable for prismatic parts. It is not suitable for rotational geometric
parts. To generate an NCS correctly on a cylinder, use the Set NCS on cylinder (page 159)

click the button in the table cell and then select a layer entity in the graphic area.

function.
Layer visible: Enter or select which layer should be visible and should therefore be evalu-
ated. The asterisk (*) symbol can be used as a wildcard. To make a selection, first click the
button in the table cell and then select a layer entity in the graphic area.
The layer visibility control is performed on reaching the function and not on executing the
function.
Restore layer setting: Restore all layer settings to the state they were in before this function
was executed.

Basic
Generate a cylindrical
hyperMILL® stock from diameter and height (or length). The stock is
aligned based on the NCS of the current job list and is inserted there. The top face of the

defined. The rest of the CAD model is not taken into account.

Find best stock (from file)
Determine the best fitting stock based on stocks stored in files.
Template commands → Stock → Find best stock (from file)
Basic
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
The character | is not allowed to be used. A * is replaced by the original command name.
Stock name: Enter a name for the stock to be generated.
Tolerance: Enter a maximum permissible distance between the faces selected for genera-
tion as initial information and the stock facets.
Stock position move Z: Shift the stock in the Z direction. Enter a value that refers to the
Top-Center reference point of the stock.
Stock offset (Z): Enter a stock offset in the +Z and -Z direction of the NCS.
Contour layer: Enter a name for a layer on which to place the lines of the contour to be
generated.
Face milling contour layer: Enter a name for a layer on which to place the Face Milling
contour to be generated. The contour is located in the Z direction at the height of the upper
side of the milling area and takes into account the size of the stock in the X and Y direction.
Stock folder: In the drop-down menu, select a folder in which different stocks are located. If
no folder exists, the option is not shown.
NOTE
The definition files must be located in a ...\STOCKS\[STOCKTYPE] fold-
er in the defined default path that is specified under hyperMILL → Setup
→ Settings in Application underDefault paths in theAUTOMATION Center
branch. If this folder does not exist, it must be created. [STOCKTYPE]
should be replaced with another name (for example, the stock group).
Stock files are *.cfg files and can be edited with a text editor and provi-
ded with various widths and heights.
Example 65. STOCK42-20-30.cfg
X_WIDTH:= 42
Y_WIDTH:= 20
HEIGHT:=30
Example 66. STOCK40-30-35.cfg
X_WIDTH:= 40
Y_WIDTH:= 30
HEIGHT:=35

Find best stock (from Excel)
Search for the stock best suited for machining in an Excel table.
Template commands → Stock → Find best stock (from Excel)
Basic
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
The character | is not allowed to be used. A * is replaced by the original command name.
Stock name: Enter a name for the stock to be generated.
Tolerance: Enter a maximum permissible distance between the faces selected for genera-
tion as initial information and the stock facets.
Stock position: Relate the stock position (+Z) to top or bottom.
Stock position move Z: Shift the stock in the Z direction. Enter a value that refers to the
Top-Center reference point of the stock.
Stock offset (Z): Enter a stock offset in the +Z and -Z direction of the NCS.
Offset XY: Enter a side offset in the X and Y direction of the NCS.
Contour layer: Enter a name for a layer on which to place the lines of the contour to be
generated.
Face milling contour layer: Enter a name for a layer on which to place the Face Milling
contour to be generated. The contour is located in the Z direction at the height of the upper
side of the milling area and takes into account the size of the stock in the X and Y direction.
Stock type: Select the type of stock.
Free stock setup: Use the stock when vertical, horizontal or rotated sideways, that is, in all
principal directions – suitable for manual setup. Or, if ‘No’ is selected, only as defined via
the length, width and height – for setup with a robot.
Folder: Select or enter a folder.
Excel file: Select an Excel file. The file folder is transferred from Folder. The stored folder is
opened if a script variable is used for Folder.
Cell address for value (start): In the Excel table, arrange the values next to each other in a
table row. Use another table row for each further definition. Select the information with the
address of the table cell (for example, A2 or A50) that contains the first value. Enter the
name of the cell here.
Sheet name: Enter the name of the Excel worksheet.
Success: Returns whether a stock was found. If not, ‘False’ is output.
Return field value: The value in the cell of the row with the stock that has been found is
output, for example, 9 for a found stock of size 100, 75, 100 1 	. The table header is of
no interest here. The fourth column after the start cell is always used – according to the
sequence X, Y, Z, Return.

Create a stock with predefined offset values.
Template commands → Stock → Auto box dimension
Basic
It is necessary to execute the Calculate stock dimensions (page 173) or Calculate stock
dimensions (parameter) (page 174) function beforehand, otherwise the result will be wrong.

calculation result and displays the calculated values.

Stock width X: Displays the size of the stock in the X direction that was calculated using the
Calculate stock dimensions function. The size can be further adjusted.
Stock width Y: Displays the size of the stock in the Y direction that was calculated using the
Calculate stock dimensions function. The size can be further adjusted.
Stock height: Displays the size of the stock in the Z direction that was calculated using the
Calculate stock dimensions function. The size can be further adjusted.

point.
Bounding box layer: Enter a name for a layer on which to place the bounding box lines to
be generated.
Face milling contour layer: Enter a name for a layer on which to place the Face Milling
contour to be generated. The contour is located in the Z direction at the height of the upper
side of the milling area and takes into account the size of the stock in the X and Y direction.
Tolerance: Enter a maximum permissible distance between the faces selected for genera-
tion as initial information and the stock facets.
Stop for input: Execution of the script is halted so that information can be entered.

Joblist ID: Select the job list for which the resulting stock is to be generated based on its job
list ID. The job list ID is a sequential number that usually arises from the order in which job
lists are created in the project.
Job range: Create the resulting stock in the current or last job list using the jobs defined in
the job range.
Define the job range in advance using either Define job range (page 315) or Multi job
selection (page 32), or define it directly. A selection made using Job range overwrites
another selection, such as a job list.
• Define an area that also contains all existing jobs within it: The following syntax is used
for the start job: SID:, for example, SID:2. The following syntax is used for the end
job: EID:, for example, EID:4. Insert a hyphen in between. This results, for example, in
SID:2-EID:4.
• List the jobs: The following syntax is used for the jobs: JID:. This results, for example, in
JID:1; JID:4;. Separate the individual jobs with a semicolon.

Fixtures Contains various functions for importing a fixture into the CAD model and defining the
hyperMILL® clamping range.
The functions build on each other and must follow a basic order in the script:
1. Prepare CAD fixture data
The data must be prepared and stored in an *.hmc file in a fixture system folder.
The following data must be determined when the data is prepared:
• Reference point as a reference between fixture and the lower edge of the milling
area or the stock or the workplane
• Non-moving parts of the fixture
• Moving parts of the fixture, for example, left and right side of a vice
• Fixture system folder and fixture name
The scripts in Template commands → Process → Setup fixture are available for prepar-
ing CAD fixture data.
2. Select fixture system
Select a folder with the required fixture system:
Select fixture system
AUTOMATION Center
180

-- 194 of 416 --

3. Load CAD fixture data
Select CAD fixture data and load into the current project using, for example:
Load fixture to stock, Load fixture to milling area or Load fixture to workplane
Or, combine with a search for the best fixture:
1. Find best fixture (from Excel)
2. Load fixture to stock (Parameter), Load fixture to milling area (Parameter) or Load
fixture to Workplane (Parameter)
4. Position fixture
Position the fixture in relation to the CAD model and clamp the CAD model:
Rotate fixture, Adjust Z level (two selected faces) and Adjust fixture to stock or Adjust
fixture to milling area
In the Manual adjust fixture function, the job steps for positioning the fixture are com-
bined in one function.
5. Specify as fixture area
Transfer as fixture area to the
hyperMILL® joblist:
Finish clamping
In contrast, the Fixture function can be used to generate a new fixture area directly from
CAD entities of the current project. The fixture area is added automatically to the last
available joblist.
Select fixture system
Select fixture system folder.
Template commands → Fixtures → Select fixture system
Basic
Before CAD fixture data can be loaded into the current project, the folder with the required
fixture system must be selected. See Load fixture to stock (page 184).
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
The character | is not allowed to be used. A * is replaced by the original command name.
Filter machine name: If No is selected, all subfolders in the .../CLAMPS folder are offered. If
Yes is selected, all subfolders in the .../CLAMPS/[MACHINETYPE] folder are offered. In this
case, organize the data in this additional folder structure.
In the active joblist, a machine must be selected in Postprocessor → NC files. The name of
the folder and the name of the machine selected in the active joblist must match.
Fixture systems: From the drop-down list, select a folder in which the different fixtures of the
various fixture systems are located.
NOTE
For each fixture system, a ...\CLAMPS\[FIXTURESYSTEMTYPE] folder
must be located in the defined default path that is specified under hy-
perMILL → Setup → Settings in Application under Default paths in the
AUTOMATION Center branch.
Stop for input: Execution of the script is halted so that information can be entered.

Find best fixture (from Excel)
Search for the best CAD fixture data with the help of an Excel table.
Template commands → Fixtures → Find best fixture (from Excel)
Basic
Search for the best CAD fixture data with the help of an Excel table. The minimum and
maximum size of the opening (ymin and ymax) and the x width (for example, of the clamping
jaws) and the name of each fixture are listed in the Excel table. A search is performed to
find the best suited fixture using this information and a search criterion. The name is output
as the result. The fixture with the smallest overhang is most suitable in X.

Fixtures
Generate a new fixture area from CAD entities of the current project.
Template commands → Fixtures → Fixtures
Basic
Generate a new fixture area from CAD entities of the current project. The entities are selec-
ted by assigning them before to specific unique layers and/or have a color only intended for
this purpose. The fixture area is added automatically to the last available joblist.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
The character | is not allowed to be used. A * is replaced by the original command name.
Fixture name: Enter the name of the fixture to load or use the script variable for the result
from the Find best fixture (from Excel) function.
Face color: Enter an RGB color value for the faces that exist in the model that are to be
included. Optionally click the button in the table cell and select a face of the respective
color. The RGB color value is transferred.
Face layer: Enter one or several layers that exist in the model whose faces should be
included. Separate multiple layer names with a semicolon ;, for example, Layer1; Layer2.
Optionally click the button in the table cell and select a face of the respective layer. The
layer name is transferred.
Tolerance: Enter a maximum permissible distance between the faceted top of the polyhe-
dron model and the real model faces. Add
| METRIC to specify that this value only applies
to metric models. This value is converted in the case of INCH models. The procedure is the
same when
| INCH is added.
Tool safe distance: Enter the minimum distance of the tool to the clamp as the clearance
distance to be maintained between the tool and the clamp.
Layer invisible: Enter or select which layer should not be visible and should therefore not
be evaluated. The asterisk (*) symbol can be used as a wildcard. To make a selection, first
click the button in the table cell and then select a layer entity in the graphic area.
The layer visibility control is performed on reaching the function and not on executing the
function.
Layer visible: Enter or select which layer should be visible and should therefore be evalu-
ated. The asterisk (*) symbol can be used as a wildcard. To make a selection, first click the
button in the table cell and then select a layer entity in the graphic area.
The layer visibility control is performed on reaching the function and not on executing the
function.

Probing points
Create probing points on contours of plane faces.
Template commands → Curves → Probing points
Advanced
2024
Create one to three points on contours, generated from a plane face. The contour may
only consist of analytical entities (no NURBS). The first point is the middle point of the limit
diameter. The second point is the one with the greatest distance to 1. The third point is the
one with the greatest distance to the line between 1 and 2.

The contour is the input information. The points are the result. The green point is the first
point. It looks for the position with the largest possible face.

Outer shape curve
Create the outer contour of a shape as a curve.
Template commands → Curves → Outer shape curve
Advanced
Create the outer contour of a shape (including mesh entities) as a curve. For the purpose
of calculating the linear as well as the rotational contour curves, the selected entities should
not describe overlapping closed volumes. Solids or faces that define closed volumes may
not intersect. At most they may touch each other.
The current workplane serves as a reference.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
The character | is not allowed to be used. A * is replaced by the original command name.

Curve layer: Enter or select a layer for the curve to be generated.
Tolerance: If the Precise option is disabled, curves are generated as polylines. Specify a
tolerance for this.
Mode: Select Linear or Rotation. If Rotation is selected, curves are generated when a
rotational contour created by rotating a shape is intersected by a plane.
Precise: Lines (such as arcs, straight lines, NURBS) are generated as the calculation result.
If the option is disabled, curves are generated as polylines.

Result: Limit diameter: The largest diameter is output with which an imaginary circle (for

a circular pocket) is output that could be inserted somewhere within the closed contour 2 	.
A target point is created in the center of the circle.
Result: Smallest inner corner radius: The smallest inner corner radius is output 3 	.
Result: Smallest outer corner radius: The smallest outer corner radius is output 4 	.

Tolerance: Two curves are normally considered to be connected when the distance be-
tween the two nearest ends is smaller that the maximum deviation (also error, precision
or accuracy) of the two curves. The tolerance can be used to join two curves where the
distance is larger that this deviation but is still smaller than the tolerance. The curves are
replaced with trimmed/extended, modified copies.

245

-- 259 of 416 --

Single point from points
Generate a point in the middle of a curve or on corners of an imaginary
bounding box.
Template commands → Curves → Single point from points
Advanced
Generate a new point on a corner of an imaginary bounding box or on the middle point,
sorted by IDs. In this way, several points can first be created on a curve in order to then
obtain the middle of the curve with the middle point. Only if an odd number of a minimum of
three points is specified.
Example 77.
1. Use the Point on curve script step in advance to generate an odd number of points on
a curve.
2. Use layer and color to select these points.
3. The ID of the middle point is used to generate a new point that lies in the middle of the
contour.
4. A bounding box is created internally around the selected points. Further points can be
created at its vertices. To do this, execute this function as often as required.
AUTOMATION Center

Target color Select a color from the active CAD color library in order to make it active.
Target layer: Enter or select a layer for the entities that are generated or modified with this
function.
Open user interface: Specify whether the user interface should be opened.
The options vary for the following functions:
Unbounded plane, Offset face, Join, Close holes, Global fitting
Face layer: Enter one or several layers that exist in the model whose faces should be
included. Separate multiple layer names with a semicolon ;, for example, Layer1; Layer2.
Optionally click the button in the table cell and select a face of the respective layer. The
layer name is transferred.
Face color: Enter an RGB color value for the faces that exist in the model that are to be
included. Optionally click the button in the table cell and select a face of the respective
color. The RGB color value is transferred.
Bounded plane, Rotational face, Face from boundary, Filling face, Through sections
Curve layer: Select the curve layer. Only uses curves that are assigned to this layer.
Curve color: Enter an RGB color value for the entities in the model that are to be included.
Optionally click the button in the table cell and select an entity of the respective color. The
RGB color value is transferred.
Linear sweep
Curve layer: Select the curve layer. Only uses curves that are assigned to this layer.
Trim faces layer: Trim the extruded faces to faces. Select or enter a layer whose faces
should be included.
Curve color: Enter an RGB color value for the entities in the model that are to be included.
Optionally click the button in the table cell and select an entity of the respective color. The
RGB color value is transferred.
Trim faces color: Trim the extruded faces to faces. Enter or select an RGB color value for
the faces that exist in the model that are to be included.
Ruled face
Curve layer 1: Select the first curves with the help of the curve layer.
Curve layer 2: Select the second curves with the help of the curve layer.
Curve color 1: Enter an RGB color value for a first section of entities in the model that are to
be included. Optionally click the button in the table cell and select an entity of the respective
color. The RGB color value is transferred.
Curve color 2: Enter an RGB color value for a second section of entities in the model that
are to be included. Optionally click the button in the table cell and select an entity of the
respective color. The RGB color value is transferred.
Along guide
Profile curve layer: Enter one or several layers that exist in the model whose curves should
be included.
Leading curve layer: Enter one or several layers that exist in the model whose curves
should be included as leading curves.
Profile curve color: Enter an RGB color value for the curves in the model that are to be
included.

Ruled face from offset curve
Generate a ruled face from a single curve by defining a distance in XY and
Z.
Template commands → Shapes → Ruled face from offset curve
Advanced
One curve is required for this.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
The character | is not allowed to be used. A * is replaced by the original command name.
Layer: Enter a layer on which the entities to be used are located. Enter a layer name or
select an entity on this layer. The ID of an entity can also be entered instead. The input
must start with ID:, for example, ID:1234.
Target layer: Enter or select a layer for the entities that are generated or modified with this
function.
Target color Enter or select a color with RGB values.
Offset XY: Enter a distance for a temporary curve in the XY direction in order to generate a
ruled face.
Offset Z: Enter a distance for a temporary curve in the Z direction in order to generate a
ruled face.
Increment: Split the length from Offset XY and Offset into intermediate stages of the length
of the increment. Enter a value for the increment.
Gap tolerance: Enter the maximum permissible distance of a gap.

Target color Enter or select a color with RGB values.
Layer invisible: Enter or select which layer should not be visible and should therefore not
be evaluated. The asterisk (*) symbol can be used as a wildcard. To make a selection, first
click the button in the table cell and then select a layer entity in the graphic area.
The layer visibility control is performed on reaching the function and not on executing the
function.
Layer visible: Enter or select which layer should be visible and should therefore be evalu-
ated. The asterisk (*) symbol can be used as a wildcard. To make a selection, first click the
button in the table cell and then select a layer entity in the graphic area.
The layer visibility control is performed on reaching the function and not on executing the
function.

range)
Template commands → Basic Programming → Feature/macro settings → Set
frame limits (3D range)
Basic
Set a frame limit. Macros are only applied if the features to be processed are located in a
defined angle range.
Use the frame limit to automatically assign jobs with hole features to a joblist. The assign-
ment is based on the direction of the hole features used in the job that can be defined using
an angle limit of the B and C axis.
Specify the permissible angle range of the B and C axis. Here, the B axis corresponds to
the Y direction and the C axis corresponds to the Z direction of the selected coordinate
system.

Hole feature recognition settings
Specify the settings for recognizing a hole feature.
Template commands → Feature → Feature/macro settings → Hole feature recog-
nition settings
Template commands → Basic Programming → Feature/macro settings → Hole
feature recognition settings
Basic
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
The character | is not allowed to be used. A * is replaced by the original command name.
Create associative point: Display and edit point coordinates of hole features relative to a
selected frame.
Grouping feature: Group together several features with identical parameters.
Frame creation mode: Choose 2D to create a feature list for each hole direction to which a
frame is assigned. Choose 5X to combine all recognized features in a feature list to which
no frame is assigned. In the case of Mixed, multiple holes have the same direction, so a
feature list is created for this direction and a frame is assigned to it. All other holes are
combined in a feature list to which no frame is assigned.
Tolerance: Specifies the accuracy level for transferring the geometric data from the CAD
model.
Slot tolerance: Enter a distance from which a feature is to be split into two or more features
if the cylinders of the geometry under investigation have the same diameters and cylinder
axis, but there is free space, for example, a slot, between them. If the distance is smaller,
the free space is bridged – only one feature is created.
Diameter limit: Enable this option when holes require a diameter limit.
Min. segment angle: All cylindrical faces with a segment angle greater than 270° are
recognized as holes. A hole that is cut open in the middle has a segment angle of 180°.
A rounded inner corner of a rectangular pocket has a segment angle of 90°. If the Min.
segment angle were set to less than 90°, every corner of a rectangular pocket with rounded
corners would be recognized as a hole.
Detect spot depth: Recognized features are checked for spots and any existing spots are
displayed accordingly.
Check bottom: Recognized features are also checked for bottom spots and any existing
spots are displayed accordingly.
AUTOMATION Center
294

-- 308 of 416 --

Check sink: A check is carried out to see if sinks can be machined from the defined
direction.
Allow NCS as reference: This option sets a global setting in the document. It therefore also
has an influence on pocket recognition.
Tag used faces: Each face used is tagged.
Also see Mark not complete cylinder hole feature faces (page 308).
Keep CAD features: Enable if a CAD feature for holes should remain associatively linked to
the CAM feature after feature mapping.

Load features
Load Customized Process Features or features (Contour, Strategy Curve,
Face Group).
Template commands → Feature → Load features
Advanced
Load Customized Process Features, abbreviated as CPF, or features (Contour, Strategy
Curve, Face Group) from a *.fst file in a subfolder of the feature file folder.
Example 80.
1. Select a feature in the
hyperMILL® browser, on the Feature tab – also called the
feature browser.
2. Right-click while holding down the CTRL key to open the context menu and select the
Export features function. Save the feature, for example, in a Allfeature.fst file in the
AUTOMATION Center feature folder.
NOTE
The ...\FEATURE\[FEATURECLASS] feature folders must be loca-
ted in the defined default path that is specified under hyperMILL →
Setup → Settings in Application under Default paths in the AUTOMA-
TION Center branch.
3. When compiling the script inAUTOMATION Center, make sure that theSelect feature
folder function is inserted as a script step before the Load features function.
4. During the script run, in the Select feature folder function in the Feature folder option,
select the subfolder created for the feature category in ...\FEATURE\.
5. Afterwards, in the Load features script step, the feature from the Allfeature.fst file is
offered in the left column of the dialog.

Load generic CPF
Change values in the filter definition of a CPF.
Template commands → Feature → Load generic CPF
Advanced
Load a CPF defined as a pattern (abbreviation for Customized Process Feature) with a
layer name adapted to the model situation. For this purpose, layer names of the exported
CPF file are changed before the file is imported and applied. This makes the CPF definition
generally usable, that is, generic.
Run the Select feature folder (page 296) function beforehand. Also see Load index CPF
(page 301).

Model1,
Model2,

Model4.

enter the layer name
Model. Export the CPF. To do this, open the context menu after
the definition by holding down the CTRL key and select the Export features function.

Input: Feature folder: OwnCPFs

Pocket recognition
Execute the
hyperMILL® Pocket recognition.

Template commands → Basic Programming → Pocket recognition
Basic
Only pockets with a bottom or through pockets are supported in one operation. The recogni-
tion direction is required for through pockets.
AUTOMATION Center
302

-- 316 of 416 --

Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
The character | is not allowed to be used. A * is replaced by the original command name.
Type: Specify which pocket shape should be recognized. The following are recognized:
• T slot
• Pocket with bottom
• Laterally open through pocket
• Complete all-round closed through pocket
Frame: Enter a frame name. Only recognizes pockets in the frame direction defined with the
frame.
Use color table: When reading feature information, pocket bottoms with a specific color
value (RGB value) are automatically recognized and the respective features are assigned to
a corresponding feature class. For this purpose, both the color value and the name of the
feature class are defined in an XML file.
Use color table only: If Use color table is selected, all pockets are detected and the pockets
that are colored with colors from the color table receive the additional parameters from the
color table. If the Use color table only option is selected, only the pockets whose color
matches colors from the color table are adopted. Pockets with colors that are not defined in
the color table are not detected.
Advanced Pocket feature recognition finished?: End for the last recognition step, other-
wise do not end in order to be able to execute separate recognition for multiple directions in
a time-effective manner.

Mark not complete cylinder hole feature faces
Mark not complete cylinder hole feature faces.
Template commands → Feature → Mark not complete cylinder hole feature
faces.
Advanced
Mark not complete cylinder hole feature faces. Incomplete features are features in which
the volume is less than the volume that would result if the feature were completely enclosed
by cylindrical faces along its entire length. This can be useful for checking whether a hole
should be made before a pocket instead of after a pocket because it is not surrounded
along its entire length by material with a wrap angle of more than 180°.
Example 85. Concept for dealing with incomplete features
1. In Hole feature recognition settings (page 294), enable the option Tag used faces.
2. Hole feature recognition.
3. Execute the Mark not complete cylinder hole feature faces (page 308) command.
Cylinder faces are identified as part of a hole, and the Tag "OM_HOLES=omHole
(Feature Mapping)" is assigned. The UUID of the relevant hole feature is assigned
to these cylinder faces. The "FeatureUUID" is set and receives the UUID of the hole
feature as a value.
Input: Feature name add: NOT COMPLETE | Feature class add: NOT COMPLETE
The incomplete feature is determined. Its feature name is supplemented with the addi-
tion "NOT COMPLETE". The feature class "NOT COMPLETE" is set.
4. Filter out features based on the feature name component that were added using Fea-
ture name add in Define feature / macro filter in order to process them differently in the
later script run.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
The character | is not allowed to be used. A * is replaced by the original command name.

Optimize jobs

Method: Select a sort sequence.
• Macro reference: The sort sequence from the macro is retained.
• Macro reference with grouping: For the sort sequence, use the job grouping for macro
jobs of the
hyperMILL® function Job Optimization.
• Joblist reference (tool-frame): Sort according to the order of the tools as the first priority.
A sensible machining sequence is assumed. A machining operation with the tools in the
sequence 1 - 2 - 3 - 4; and a second machining operation in the sequence 1 - 2 - 3 - 4;
results in a sequence 1 - 1; 2 - 2; 3 - 3; 4 - 4;
• Joblist reference (frame-tool): Sort by order of frames as the first priority.
Renumber joblist: Adapt the numbering to the new sequence.
