Exact collision check (SIMULATION Center)
Perform a collision check.
Template commands → Programming → Exact collision check (SIMULATION
Center)
Template commands → Basic Programming → Exact collision check (SIMULA-
TION Center)
Basic
Perform a collision check without displaying a simulation. The collision check is performed
according to the options saved for the collision check. Collisions found are classified. It
is possible to configure which types of collisions are to be reported/are reported. In case
of collision, start either the VIRTUAL Machining Center or the SIMULATION Center. If a
postprocessor is selected where the Virtual Machine option is not set, the SIMULATION
Center will be started. If the option is set, the VIRTUAL Machining Center is started.
Definitions
The following applies to the terms used in the collision check message:
Collision: Collision between part, stock, fixture and machine or the non-cutting part of the
tool (the tolerance values are defined in the dialog Collision check options → Limits for part
contact). An NC program with collisions cannot be approved.
Gouge: Violation of the part with the cutting area of the tool. An NC program with part
violations cannot be approved.
Contact: Permitted violation of the part with the tool tip (the tolerance values are defined in
the dialog Collision check options → Limits for part contact). An NC program with contacts
can be approved.
AUTOMATION Center
321

-- 335 of 416 --

IMPORTANT
If the cutting edge length is 0, the length of the tool tip corresponds to the
cutting tool part. If the cutting edge length is not equal to 0, the length of
the tool tip without cutting edge corresponds to the non-cutting tool part.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
The character | is not allowed to be used. A * is replaced by the original command name.
Joblist: Select a joblist for using the function.
Job range: Perform the calculation using the jobs defined in the job range of the current or
last job list.
Define the job range in advance using either Define job range (page 315) or Multi job
selection (page 32), or define it directly. A selection made using Job range overwrites
another selection, such as a job list.
• Define an area that also contains all existing jobs within it: The following syntax is used
for the start job: SID:, for example, SID:2. The following syntax is used for the end
job: EID:, for example, EID:4. Insert a hyphen in between. This results, for example, in
SID:2-EID:4.
• List the jobs: The following syntax is used for the jobs: JID:. This results, for example, in
JID:1; JID:4;. Separate the individual jobs with a semicolon.
CHECK: gouge: Report whether collisions have occurred that are classified as a component
violation. In a component violation, the cutting part of the (milling) tool causes damage to
the workpiece, for example, in the case of an infeed via the part. This renders the workpiece
unusable.
CHECK: touch: Report whether collisions have occurred that are classified as a component
contact. The tool tip (cutting area, non-cutting area of the tool, core) touches the workpiece.
Note: This collision check is only performed if the Limits for component contact are set in
the collision check options.
CHECK: collision: Report whether other collisions have occurred, that is, collisions that
cannot be classified as a component contact or component violation.
CHECK: limits: Report whether machining can be carried out within the axis limits, that is,
whether production is possible in the selected clamping position.
Start VM in case of danger: If at least one of the four checked collisions occurs, the
VIRTUAL Machining Center or SIMULATION Center software is started and the data is
loaded. Investigate the situation interactively.
Message: Enter message text for ‘Collision detected’.
Message handling: Select Display, Write to File, Add to File or Ignore.
Folder: Select or enter a folder.
Filename: Select or enter a file name.
Is collision free?: ‘True’ is output if no collisions were found. ‘False’ is displayed in the event
of collisions.

Prepare VT XML for AC
Prepare a *.vtxVirtual Tool file with XML structures for the AUTOMATION
Center.
Template commands → Tool → Prepare VT XML for AC
Advanced
VT stands for ‘Virtual Tool’. The *.vtx from the Virtual Tool is supplemented with data
structures and so prepared for processing in the AUTOMATION Center.
Example 87. Overview of the arrangement of functions for optimizing the tool projection
length.
Optimization of the tool projection length to avoid tool holder collisions, as in here for
example, for a hole inside a pocket.
AUTOMATION Center
324

-- 338 of 416 --

In preparation, a user database project with a macro database and a Virtual Tool must be
entered in hyperMILL → Setup → Settings in Database.
1. Execute Prepare VT XML for AC (page 324). The *.vtx from the Virtual Tool is sup-
plemented with data structures and so prepared for processing in the AUTOMATION
Center.
2. Then, for example, run a hole feature recognition process and assign a macro. Drilling
jobs are created.
3. Execute Prepare tools and jobs for minimal tool length (page 328). In
hyperMILL® in
the job, in Settings in Calculate length for a tool, the option Reduce is activated. The
specified imaginary tool length is set for all jobs or only for the jobs with a collision
warning.
4. Recalculate jobs. In the newly calculated jobs, the imaginary tools are assigned the
minimum projection length that has now been reduced again.
5. Execute Prepare tools and jobs for minimal tool length (page 328). In
hyperMILL® in
the job, in Settings in Calculate length for a tool, deactivate the Reduce option.
6. Execute Calculated minimal tool length to job CGF (page 327). It goes through the
jobs. The minimum projection length is transferred to the CFG parameter of the respec-
tive job.
7. Execute Optimize tools (VT) (page 325). A suitable tool is searched for from the tool
database with the help of the *.vtx extended for the AUTOMATION Center.
8. Recalculate jobs.
9. Delete data that is no longer required.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
The character | is not allowed to be used. A * is replaced by the original command name.
Folder: Enter or select a folder.
Filename VT: Enter the *.vtx file entered in the
hyperMILL® settings here as well.

minimum projection length that has now been reduced again.
5. Execute Prepare tools and jobs for minimal tool length (page 328). In
hyperMILL® in
the job, in Settings in Calculate length for a tool, deactivate the Reduce option.
6. Execute Calculated minimal tool length to job CGF (page 327). It goes through the
jobs. The minimum projection length is transferred to the CFG parameter of the respec-
tive job.
7. Execute Optimize tools (VT) (page 325). A suitable tool is searched for from the tool
database with the help of the *.vtx extended for the AUTOMATION Center.
8. Recalculate jobs.
9. Delete data that is no longer required.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
The character | is not allowed to be used. A * is replaced by the original command name.
The fixed CFG parameter NEEDED_TOOL_LENGTH is used to pass the calculated mini-
mum tool length to the jobs.

Example 91. Overview of the arrangement of functions for optimizing the tool projection
length.
Optimization of the tool projection length to avoid tool holder collisions, as in here for
example, for a hole inside a pocket.
In preparation, a user database project with a macro database and a Virtual Tool must be
entered in hyperMILL → Setup → Settings in Database.
1. Execute Prepare VT XML for AC (page 324). The *.vtx from the Virtual Tool is sup-
plemented with data structures and so prepared for processing in the AUTOMATION
Center.
2. Then, for example, run a hole feature recognition process and assign a macro. Drilling
jobs are created.
3. Execute Prepare tools and jobs for minimal tool length (page 328). In
hyperMILL® in
the job, in Settings in Calculate length for a tool, the option Reduce is activated. The
specified imaginary tool length is set for all jobs or only for the jobs with a collision
warning.
4. Recalculate jobs. In the newly calculated jobs, the imaginary tools are assigned the
minimum projection length that has now been reduced again.

hyperMILL® in
the job, in Settings in Calculate length for a tool, deactivate the Reduce option.

tive job.
7. Execute Optimize tools (VT) (page 325). A suitable tool is searched for from the tool
database with the help of the *.vtx extended for the AUTOMATION Center.
8. Recalculate jobs.
9. Delete data that is no longer required.

Test tool length: Enter an imaginary tool length that is at least the length of the maximum

Enable reduce tool length: In
hyperMILL® in the job, in Settings in Calculate length for a
tool, deactivate or deactivate the Reduce option.
Filter jobs: Perform the calculation for all jobs or only for those jobs for which a warning
about collision (of the tool holder) with the model has been issued.

Execute VBS script
Execute a VBS script.
Template commands → External automation → Execute VBS script
Advanced
To manage complex requirements, an externally generated script can be executed in
the scripting language VBScript, also called VBS (also see, for example, https://en.wikipe-
dia.org/wiki/VBScript). The software program necessary comes with the operating system –
it can be found in %windir%/system32/wscript.exe". The user does therefore not need to do
anything.
Use the Choose VBS script folder (page 333) function to select the storage location of the
VBS scripts in advance.
Example 92. Determine whether a numerical value is greater than, equal to or less than
another numerical value.
Input:
Description VBS Parameter ‘P1’: Value1 | Description VBS Parameter ‘P2’: Value2
Value1: 25 | Value2: 50
Software program:
REM VBscript example
If P1>P2 Then
returnVal1= "The value "+ P1+ " is bigger then "+ P2
ElseIf P1=P2 Then
AUTOMATION Center
333

-- 347 of 416 --

returnVal1= "The value "+ P1+ " is equal then "+ P2
Else
returnVal1= "The value "+ P1+ " is less then "+ P2
End if
returnVal2="returnVal2 is currently not used"
returnVal3="returnVal3 is currently not used"
returnVal4="returnVal4 is currently not used"
Results:
Return from script: The value 25 is less then 50
Return from script: returnVal2 is currently not used
Return from script: returnVal3 is currently not used
Return from script: returnVal4 is currently not used
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
The character | is not allowed to be used. A * is replaced by the original command name.
VBS scripts: Select a VBS script from the drop-down menu.
If this option is not offered, the VBS script folder has not been selected. Use the button at
the end of the line to open the VBS script in a text editor.
Description VBS Parameter 'P1' toDescription VBS Parameter 'P20': First enter a descrip-
tion for the parameters. A value can then be entered during the next run.
Pass up to 20 parameters to the VBS script. For processing, the variables P1 to P20 must
be used in the VBS script, whereby the value from Description VBS Parameter ‘P1’ is
passed to P1 and so on.
Return from script: The results of the calculation in the function. Transfer to other functions
with the help of the script variables. To do this, the results must be passed within the VBS
script to variables with the names returnVal1 to returnVal4.

Software program:
# Python script example
if P1 > P2:
returnVal1= "The value "+ str(P1)+ " is bigger then "+ str(P2)
elif P1 == P2:
returnVal1= "The value "+ str(P1)+ " is equal then "+ str(P2)
else:
returnVal1= "The value "+ str(P1)+ " is less then "+ str(P2)
returnVal2="returnVal2 is currently not used"
returnVal3="returnVal3 is currently not used"
returnVal4="returnVal4 is currently not used"
Results:
Return from script: The value 25 is less then 50
Return from script: returnVal2 is currently not used
Return from script: returnVal3 is currently not used
Return from script: returnVal4 is currently not used

Call subprogram script
Continue with another script. Call a script as a subroutine.
Template commands → External automation → Call subprogram script
Template commands → Basic Programming → Call subprogram script
Basic
After the script has been called, the process continues in the parent script. In general, a
check is made at the end of each script run to see whether it was called as a subprogram
from a parent script. Where necessary, the process returns to the position of the calling
script.
Up to 20 values can be passed to the child process. The parameter names of these values
are $FUNCTION_PARAM1# to $FUNCTION_PARAM20#.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.

Script name: Enter a script name as it appears in the AUTOMATION Center in the drop-
down menu for script selection.
Subscript: Select a subscript to be executed if a subscript is defined within the selected
script.
For up to 20 parameters, enter the descriptions of the parameters in the dialog that (for the
first time in a second step) are added to the first column of the table as names and can be
assigned values:
This is useful if a subscript is not to be used with global parameters of the master script, but
repeatedly with other values in the script run, for example, in a loop.
Parameter 1 description to Parameter 20 description: Enter short descriptions for the pa-
rameters.

Create NC file
Execute the
hyperMILL® Create NC file function for the last joblist or all joblists.
Template commands → Finish → Create NC file
Template commands → Basic Programming → Create NC file
Basic
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
The character | is not allowed to be used. A * is replaced by the original command name.
Joblist: Select a joblist for using the function.
Job range: Perform the calculation using the jobs defined in the job range of the current or
last job list.
Define the job range in advance using either Define job range (page 315) or Multi job
selection (page 32), or define it directly. A selection made using Job range overwrites
another selection, such as a job list.
• Define an area that also contains all existing jobs within it: The following syntax is used
for the start job: SID:, for example, SID:2. The following syntax is used for the end
job: EID:, for example, EID:4. Insert a hyphen in between. This results, for example, in
SID:2-EID:4.
• List the jobs: The following syntax is used for the jobs: JID:. This results, for example, in
JID:1; JID:4;. Separate the individual jobs with a semicolon.

Stop immediately.
Running the AUTOMATION Center on a server
The AUTOMATION Center can be run easily on a server. The script must be adapted so
that there is no user interaction, otherwise the application will fail. It is very important to
know that the application only monitors one folder and executes a script that has been de-
fined beforehand. Every action such as ‘save file’, ‘log message’, ‘error’ must be managed
in the script. The application only executes what has been requested.
All files can be found in C:\Program Files\OPEN MIND\hyperMILL\[version]\addins\hmAuto-
Color.
Configuring the PW Server (PWserver.exe)
An executable file must be defined:
• Watch folder
• Project number (script to be executed)
• Priority
• File type
The Watch folder must be a folder that is accessible to all clients and the server.
Configuring the PW Monitor (PWMonitor.exe)
The PW Monitor can be used to display some information to the client during the process.
The log file must have the extension *.PWlog. The PW Monitor is started by the client.
AUTOMATION Center
350

-- 364 of 416 --

The script manages everything that is entered into the PW Monitor and when it is entered.

server in order to run.
The application can be started in the following order:

Using the PW Server with Excel files
Configuration file
Before starting, some parameters have to be defined. These parameters can be defined
in the autoProgramConfig.pcf file. This configuration file is located at C:\Programme\OPEN
MIND\hyperMILL\[Version]\addins\hmAutoColor. In this example, the version is 29.0.
Three parameters must be defined:
*EXCEL_FOLDER=c:/temp/defaultExcel.xlsx
*DEFAULT_TEMPLATE=c:/temp/HCS_template.hmc
*DEFAULT_TEMP_START_FILE=c:/temp/start.hmc
The Excel file located in the server’s internal folder is copied to the name defined in the
*EXCEL_FOLDER parameter.
The file defined in *DEFAULT_TEMPLATE is copied to the file defined in *DE-
FAULT_TEMP_START_FILE.
hyperMILL® is started with *DEFAULT_TEMP_START_FILE.
In the script, the user should read the Excel file from *EXCEL_FOLDER. It is assumed that
a script name has been defined for the process. It must be called from the start script.
A model file is to be found in Excel that should be merged with the current model.
The file should be saved in a location defined in the Excel file.
The file must be edited as administrator.
The file configured as an example:
hmct document template
It is possible to define a
hyperMILL® template to be used in the process. Define layers,
colors and so on in this template. The AUTOMATION Center copies the template to DE-
FAULT_START_FILE and starts based on this file.
AUTOMATION Center
357

-- 371 of 416 --

Template folder
Only the file defined in DEFAULT_TEMPLATE can be saved in this folder.
Excel file
Example: Three parameters defined in the Excel file
• Use File path to define the location of the 3D model to be merged.
• Use File to define the 3D model to be merged.
• Use Script to define the script to be started.

Excel calculation Contains functions for passing values to Microsoft Excel for the purpose of performing
calculations there or for importing data from Microsoft Excel.
For example, in the function Write value in Excel, why not ‘...save’?
A file is opened and data is read out. Conversely, data is written to Excel. The file is only
saved once all the data has been written. The file is opened automatically if it has not
already been opened. There is a function for closing the file.

Example 97. Color faces differently based on tag information.
In a *.hmc there are four faces of the same color to which tags are assigned.
1. Determine how many different values occur for the ‘T_ROU_PMETHOD’ tag. To do
this, insert the Read all tag values function into a script and execute it.
2. Insert a component into the script and configure it as a counted loop. For the number of
runs, take the result from step 1.
a. Run the Get single tag values function.
b. Run the Change values by decision table function.
c. Run the Change color or layer function.
Input: Tag name: T_ROU_PMETHOD | Tag value: Retrieve the result from individ-
ual tag value, for example, M14 | Target color: Retrieve the result from Change
values by decision table, for example, 128;0;128
The four faces are displayed in a different color.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
The character | is not allowed to be used. A * is replaced by the original command name.
Number: Use §CURRENT_LOOP_INDEX# to control which value is to be processed.
Result: The result of the calculation in the function. Transfer to other functions with the help
of the script variable.

Example 108. Determine number of tools
Determine how many tools are contained in an OPEN MIND tool database file1 to control,
for example, the number of passes of a tool loop.
Input: Segment: tool
Result: Result: 4
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<omtdx>
<coolants />
<cuttingMaterials />
<couplings/>
<inserts />
<toolHolders />

Example 110. Read the value for the distance between two points
The function Dump two entities in XML (page 390) was used to output information about
geometric properties of two points in relation to each other to a D:\AC\Result.xml file. Now
the distance between the two points is to be read in.
Input: Folder: D:\AC | File name: Result.xml | Segment: property| Search name: MinDi-
stance | Search parameter: |
for
<PBag name="info.twoentities.description"
version="1">
<property name="Deltax"
type="double"
uuid="d46df12d-5e3b-53c1-bb37-6d6b1e3223f9"
dimension="Length">10</property>
<property name="Deltay"
type="double"
uuid="d46df12d-5e3b-53c1-bb37-6d6b1e3223f9"
dimension="Length">0</property>
<property name="Deltaz"
type="double"
uuid="d46df12d-5e3b-53c1-bb37-6d6b1e3223f9"
dimension="Length">0</property>
<property name="MinDistance"
type="double"
uuid="d46df12d-5e3b-53c1-bb37-6d6b1e3223f9"
dimension="Length">10</property>
</PBag>
Result: Result: 10
Example 111. Narrow down the search to segment using two-stage search
The Write feature to XML file (page 306) function was used to output information on CAM
features to a D:\AC\Exported_features.xml file. The task now is to determine a property of a
feature in this file.
01 <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8" standalone="yes"?>
02 <Features>
03 <Feature catalog="OM" type="Hole"
name="Drill Hole" uuid="19E92F77-3D9E">
04 <Parameter name="Hole::Diameter" value="10"/>
05 <Parameter name="Hole::Depth" value="50"/>
06 <Parameter name="Bottom_Type" value="Through"/>
07 <Parameter name="Bottom_Offset" value="0"/>
08 <Parameter name="Orientation" value="0,-1,0"/>
09 <Parameter name="Position" value="7.2,-25,16.1"/>
10 </Feature>
11 <Feature catalog="OM" type="Hole"
name="Drill Hole" uuid="EEDB0AC0-94E2">
12 <Parameter name="Hole::Diameter" value="10"/>

1. Insert the Read value from XML file function into the script and execute it.

parameter: uuid | Search name: 1E6B8223-9108
Results: Found in line: 19
2. Insert the Read value from XML file function into the script a second time and execute
it.
Input: Folder: D:\AC | File name: Exported_features.xml | Start search line: 19 | Seg-
ment: Parameter | Search parameter: Name | Search name: Orientation | Get parame-
ter: value
Results: Result: 0,0,1 | Found in line: 24

is used as the name in the script.
